```ts
// e2e/global.setup.spec.ts -- run once: a fresh environment, reference data, and a storage state.
import { test as setup, expect } from '@playwright/test'
import { mkdir, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises'
import { E2E_KEY } from './config/env'

const ADMIN_STATE = 'e2e/.auth/admin.json'

setup('prepare the environment', async ({ playwright, baseURL }) => {
  // 1. Wait for the app to be genuinely ready, not merely listening.
  await expect(async () => {
    const res = await fetch(`${baseURL}/healthz`)
    expect(res.ok).toBe(true)
  }).toPass({ timeout: 120_000, intervals: [500, 1000, 2000] })

  // 2. Ensure the API version the suite expects. Fail fast with a clear message.
  const info = await (await fetch(`${baseURL}/api/version`)).json()
  if (info.apiVersion !== process.env.EXPECTED_API_VERSION) {
    throw new Error(`E2E expects API ${process.env.EXPECTED_API_VERSION}, environment serves ${info.apiVersion}`)
  }

  // 3. One authenticated profile, reused by every test that needs an admin.
  await mkdir('e2e/.auth', { recursive: true })
  const context = await playwright.chromium.launchPersistentContext('', { baseURL })
  const page = await context.newPage()
  await page.goto('/sign-in')
  await page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /email address/i }).fill('admin@example.test')
  await page.getByLabel(/^password$/i).fill('e2e-admin-password')
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: /sign in/i }).click()
  await expect(page.getByRole('banner')).toContainText(/admin/i)
  await context.storageState({ path: ADMIN_STATE })
  await context.close()
})

export { ADMIN_STATE }
```

```ts
// e2e/chaos.spec.ts -- degraded network, with a REAL fault (not route.abort).
import { test, expect } from './fixtures/test'
import { CARD } from './fixtures/journeys'
import { createChaosProxy } from './helpers/chaos-proxy'
import { BASE_URL } from './config/env'

test.describe('degraded network', () => {
  test('a reset connection surfaces an error and preserves the basket', async ({ signedIn, shopper, api, asMember }) => {
    // A real ECONNRESET on the payment call.
    await signedIn.route('**/api/payment-intent', (route) => route.abort('connectionreset'))

    await shopper.addToBasket('Widget').openBasket().checkout().payWithCard(CARD).placeOrder()

    await expect(signedIn.getByRole('alert')).toContainText(/could not complete the payment/i)
    await expect(signedIn.getByRole('button', { name: /try again/i })).toBeEnabled()
    expect(await api.countOrders(asMember.id)).toBe(0)
    await expect(signedIn.getByLabel(/card number/i)).toHaveValue(/4242/)   // the user did not retype anything
  })

  test('offline shows a banner and the basket is still browsable', async ({ signedIn, page, shopper }) => {
    await shopper.addToBasket('Widget').openBasket()
    await page.context().setOffline(true)
    await page.evaluate(() => window.dispatchEvent(new Event('offline')))

    await expect(page.getByRole('status', { name: /you are offline/i })).toBeVisible()
    await expect(page.getByRole('row', { name: /widget/i })).toBeVisible()
    await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: /checkout/i })).toBeDisabled()   // cannot pay offline
  })
})
```

**What this E2E tier demonstrates.** Per-test namespacing with a guaranteed purge in a `finally` (which is what makes `fullyParallel` safe); setup through the API rather than the UI; capability objects rather than page-per-screen; negative assertions against the system of record (`countOrders === 0` on failure paths); a `@smoke` tag for the post-deploy gate; accessibility assertions through axe on the assembled page; degraded-network journeys including a real connection reset; trace/video/screenshot capture on failure so triage is minutes rather than hours; and a quarantine registry that is counted in CI rather than hidden.

---

## PROBLEM 7: A k6 load test script

**What you must build.** A k6 script with a realistic user journey, a staged arrival model (warm-up, peak hold, spike), SLO thresholds as hard gates, business-correctness checks under load, environment-specific threshold files, and a CI regression profile.

```bash
# k6 is a single binary: https://github.com/grafana/k6/releases
k6 version
```

```js
// perf/checkout.js -- the full scenario: stages, thresholds, business checks, correctness guards.
import http from 'k6/http'
import exec from 'k6/execution'
import { check, group, sleep, fail } from 'k6'
import { Trend, Rate, Counter, Gauge } from 'k6/metrics'
import { randomIntBetween } from 'https://jslib.k6.io/k6-utils/1.4.0/index.js'

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Custom metrics. Business outcomes, not just HTTP. A 200-OK rate on an empty
// order is not a passing load test.
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
const orderCreated = new Trend('order_created_duration', true)
const checkoutAttempt = new Trend('checkout_attempt_duration', true)
const checkoutFailure = new Rate('checkout_failure_rate')
const declineHandled = new Counter('decline_handled_correctly')
const correctnessViolation = new Counter('correctness_violation')     // MUST be 0
const amountMismatch = new Counter('amount_mismatch')                 // MUST be 0
const journeyOk = new Counter('journey_complete')

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Configuration
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
const BASE_URL = __ENV.BASE_URL || 'http://127.0.0.1:3100'
const THINK_TIME = Number(__ENV.THINK_TIME || 1)
const EXPECTED_TOTAL_CENTS = 7498      // 2 x 1250 + 4999, computed by the server
const SETTLE_TOLERANCE = 0.02          // 2%: not a business assertion, a canary

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// The load model. Staged, so every phase answers a different question.
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
export const options = {
  scenarios: {
    peak: {
      executor: 'ramping-vus',
      gracefulStop: '30s',
      startVUs: 0,
      stages: [
        { duration: '1m',  target: 20 },    // warm-up: JIT, connection pools, caches
        { duration: '5m',  target: 50 },    // ramp to the expected daily peak
        { duration: '10m', target: 50 },    // HOLD: only this phase's metrics are steady-state valid
        { duration: '2m',  target: 0 },     // drain
      ],
      exec: 'checkoutJourney',
      tags: { scenario: 'peak' },
    },

    // A marketing email lands: a 6x instantaneous jump.
    spike: {
      executor: 'ramping-vus',
      startVUs: 50,
      startTime: '12m',
      stages: [
        { duration: '5s',  target: 300 },   // the shock
        { duration: '2m',  target: 300 },   // sustained spike
        { duration: '3m',  target: 50 },    // recovery: does it come back?
      ],
      exec: 'checkoutJourney',
      tags: { scenario: 'spike' },
    },
  },

  thresholds: {
    // ── Latency. Percentiles only; the mean hides the tail that users complain about.
    'http_req_duration{scenario:peak}':  ['p(90)<250', 'p(95)<400', 'p(99)<1200'],
    'http_req_duration{scenario:spike}': ['p(95)<900', 'p(99)<2500'],
    'http_req_waiting{scenario:peak}':   ['p(95)<250'],   // queueing inside the app
    'http_req_connect{scenario:peak}':   ['p(95)<50'],    // connection pool sizing
    'http_req_blocked':                  ['p(95)<50'],    // the GENERATOR is not the bottleneck

    // ── Errors: the SLO's error budget.
    'http_req_failed{scenario:peak}':    ['rate<0.01'],
    'checkout_failure_rate':             ['rate<0.005'],

    // ── Business SLOs.
    'checkout_attempt_duration':         ['p(95)<800', 'p(99)<2000'],
    'order_created_duration':            ['p(95)<600'],
    'checks':                            ['rate>0.995'],

    // ── Correctness under load. A fast, wrong system is a failed load test.
    'correctness_violation':             ['count==0'],
    'amount_mismatch':                   ['count==0'],

    // ── Generator health. If these fail, none of the numbers above mean anything.
    'vus':                               ['max>=50'],
    'dropped_iterations':                ['count==0'],

    // ── Abort early if the system is clearly broken rather than collecting 15 minutes of garbage.
    'http_req_failed{scenario:peak}': [{
      threshold: 'rate>0.10', abortOnFail: true, delayAbortEval: '45s',
    }],
  },

  // ── Realistic transport: keep-alive on, so we measure the app's pool, not TCP setup.
  noConnectionReuse: false,
  insecureSkipTLSVerify: __ENV.TLS_SELF_SIGNED === 'true',
  summaryTrendStats: ['avg', 'min', 'med', 'p(90)', 'p(95)', 'p(99)', 'p(99.9)', 'max'],
  summaryTimeUnit: 's',
  discardResponseBodies: false,   // we assert on bodies, so we need them
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Setup: create the world ONCE. Never inside the VU loop.
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
export function setup() {
  const health = http.get(`${BASE_URL}/healthz`, { tags: { phase: 'setup' } })
  if (health.status !== 200) fail(`service unhealthy: ${health.status} ${health.body}`)

  const users = []
  const USER_COUNT = Number(__ENV.LOAD_USERS || 300)
  for (let i = 0; i < USER_COUNT; i++) {
    const r = http.post(
      `${BASE_URL}/api/test/load/users`,
      JSON.stringify({ index: i, namespace: __ENV.RUN_ID || 'k6' }),
      { headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', 'x-e2e-key': __ENV.E2E_KEY }, tags: { phase: 'setup' } },
    )
    if (r.status !== 201) fail(`user seed failed at ${i}: ${r.status} ${r.body}`)
    users.push(r.json('id'))
  }
  // Realistic catalogue: 200 SKUs, so the DB is not trivially small and caches behave realistically.
  http.post(`${BASE_URL}/api/test/load/catalog`,
    JSON.stringify({ count: 200, namespace: __ENV.RUN_ID || 'k6' }),
    { headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', 'x-e2e-key': __ENV.E2E_KEY }, tags: { phase: 'setup' } })

  return { users, startedAt: Date.now(), runId: __ENV.RUN_ID || 'k6' }
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// The journey. A realistic multi-step flow, not a single endpoint. A load test that
// only GETs will never find a write-concurrency or a transaction bug.
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
export function checkoutJourney(data) {
  const userId = data.users[__VU % data.users.length]
  const headers = {
    'Content-Type': 'application/json',
    Authorization: `Bearer ${__ENV.TOKEN}`,
    'x-load-test': 'true',                       // so analytics and billing can exclude it
    'x-e2e-namespace': data.runId,
  }

  // ── 1. Browse. Most real sessions are reads.
  const browse = http.get(`${BASE_URL}/api/products?limit=24&page=1`, { headers, tags: { step: 'browse' } })
  check(browse, {
    'browse: 200': (r) => r.status === 200,
    'browse: has items': (r) => {
      const items = r.json('items')
      if (!Array.isArray(items) || items.length === 0) { correctnessViolation.add(1); return false }
      return true
    },
    'browse: honours the limit': (r) => (r.json('items') || []).length <= 24,
  })

  // ── 2. Product detail.
  const sku = `SKU-${(__VU % 200) + 1}`
  const detail = http.get(`${BASE_URL}/api/products/${sku}`, { headers, tags: { step: 'detail' } })
  check(detail, { 'detail: 200 or 404': (r) => r.status === 200 || r.status === 404 })

  // ── 3. Add to basket, then read the basket. Exercises the write path and the read-after-write.
  const add = http.post(`${BASE_URL}/api/cart/items`,
    JSON.stringify({ sku, qty: 1 }),
    { headers, tags: { step: 'add-to-basket' } })
  check(add, { 'add: 200 or 201': (r) => r.status === 200 || r.status === 201 })

  const cart = http.get(`${BASE_URL}/api/cart`, { headers, tags: { step: 'read-cart' } })
  check(cart, {
    'cart: 200': (r) => r.status === 200,
    'cart: read-after-write is consistent': (r) => {
      const items = r.json('items') || []
      if (!items.some((i) => i.sku === sku)) { correctnessViolation.add(1); return false }
      return true
    },
  })

  // ── 4. Checkout, with a 5% decline rate so the failure path is exercised under load too.
  const willDecline = (__ITER + __VU) % 20 === 0
  const started = Date.now()
  const checkout = http.post(`${BASE_URL}/api/orders`,
    JSON.stringify({ userId, cardToken: willDecline ? 'tok_decline' : 'tok_visa', lines: [{ sku, qty: 1 }] }),
    { headers: { ...headers, 'Idempotency-Key': `k6-${__VU}-${__ITER}` }, tags: { step: 'checkout' } })
  checkoutAttempt.add(Date.now() - started)

  const outcome = check(checkout, {
    'checkout: 201': (r) => r.status === 201,
    'checkout: 402 for a decline': (r) => !(willDecline ? r.status !== 402 : false),
    'checkout: has an order id': (r) => {
      if (r.status !== 201) return true
      if (!r.json('id')) { correctnessViolation.add(1); return false }
      return true
    },
    'checkout: total is plausible': (r) => {
      if (r.status !== 201) return true
      const total = r.json('totalCents')
      // The server's total must be within a few percent of the catalogue price, or the DB
      // is returning garbage under concurrency. This is a canary, not a business rule.
      const expected = 1250 + 4999
      if (typeof total !== 'number' || Math.abs(total - expected) / expected > SETTLE_TOLERANCE) {
        amountMismatch.add(1)
        return false
      }
      return true
    },
  })

  if (checkout.status === 402) declineHandled.add(1)
  if (checkout.status === 201) orderCreated.add(checkout.timings.duration)
  checkoutFailure.add(!outcome)
  if (checkout.status === 201) journeyOk.add(1)

  // ── 5. Order history, a read of a growing table.
  const orders = http.get(`${BASE_URL}/api/orders?limit=20`, { headers, tags: { step: 'orders' } })
  check(orders, { 'orders: 200': (r) => r.status === 200 })

  // Think time makes this a *realistic* load. Omit it for a pure saturation/capacity test.
  sleep(Math.random() * THINK_TIME)
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Teardown: the invariants the HTTP thresholds cannot express.
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
export function teardown(data) {
  const mins = Math.round((Date.now() - data.startedAt) / 60000)
  const ok = journeyOk.count
  const declined = declineHandled.count
  const total = ok + declined
  console.log(`
── k6 summary ───────────────────────────────────────────────
  duration           ${mins} min
  journeys completed ${total}
  succeeded          ${ok} (${total ? ((ok / total) * 100).toFixed(2) : '0'}%)
  declines handled   ${declined} (${total ? ((declined / total) * 100).toFixed(2) : '0'}%)   expected ~5%
  correctness faults ${correctnessViolation.count}   (must be 0)
  amount mismatches  ${amountMismatch.count}   (must be 0)
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────`)

  if (correctnessViolation.count > 0 || amountMismatch.count > 0) {
    fail('CORRECTNESS VIOLATION under load -- the run is invalid regardless of latency')
  }
  // A decline rate far from 5% means the payment path collapsed, which a 200-rate would miss.
  if (total > 100 && declined / total < 0.02) {
    console.warn('::warning:: decline rate far below the seeded 5% -- the decline path may not be running')
  }
}
```

```js
// perf/smoke.js -- the every-PR profile. Short, cheap, and still an SLO gate.
import { check, sleep, group } from 'k6'
import http from 'k6/http'

const BASE_URL = __ENV.BASE_URL
const headers = { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', Authorization: `Bearer ${__ENV.TOKEN}`, 'x-load-test': 'true' }

export const options = {
  vus: 25,
  duration: '45s',
  thresholds: {
    http_req_duration: ['p(95)<600', 'p(99)<1500'],
    http_req_failed: ['rate<0.02'],
    checks: ['rate>0.99'],
    http_req_blocked: ['p(95)<50'],
  },
  summaryTrendStats: ['p(95)', 'p(99)', 'max'],
}

export function smoke() {
  group('read path', () => {
    const res = http.get(`${BASE_URL}/api/products?limit=24`, { headers })
    check(res, { 'products 200': (r) => r.status === 200 })
  })
  group('write path', () => {
    const res = http.post(`${BASE_URL}/api/cart/items`, JSON.stringify({ sku: 'SKU-1', qty: 1 }), { headers })
    check(res, { 'add to cart 200/201': (r) => r.status === 200 || r.status === 201 })
  })
  sleep(0.5)
}
```

```bash
# ── Local, against a local stack
k6 run -e BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3100 -e THINK_TIME=0.2 --vus 20 --duration 30s perf/smoke.js

# ── Staging: the real profile, human output plus a machine report
k6 run \
  -e BASE_URL=https://staging.example.test \
  -e THINK_TIME=1 \
  -e RUN_ID="k6-$(date +%s)" \
  --out json=perf/results-$(date +%s).json \
  perf/checkout.js

# ── CI regression gate against a preview environment
k6 run -e BASE_URL="$PREVIEW_URL" -e TOKEN="$E2E_TOKEN" -e E2E_KEY="$E2E_KEY" perf/smoke.js

# ── Distributed generation, when one machine is not enough. Same script, disjoint segments.
k6 run --execution-segment 0/3 -e BASE_URL=$URL perf/checkout.js &
k6 run --execution-segment 1/3 -e BASE_URL=$URL perf/checkout.js &
k6 run --execution-segment 2/3 -e BASE_URL=$URL perf/checkout.js &
wait
```

```js
// perf/report.mjs -- turn the JSON into a trend the team actually reads.
import { readFileSync, readdirSync } from 'node:fs'

const files = readdirSync('perf/results').filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json'))
const series = files.map((f) => {
  const lines = readFileSync(`perf/results/${f}`, 'utf8').trim().split('\n').map(JSON.parse)
  const durations = lines.filter((l) => l.type === 'Point' && l.metric === 'http_req_duration{scenario:peak}')
  const values = durations.map((d) => d.data.value).sort((a, b) => a - b)
  const pct = (p) => values[Math.floor(values.length * p)] ?? 0
  return { run: f.replace(/\.json$/, ''), p95: pct(0.95), p99: pct(0.99), n: values.length }
}).sort((a, b) => a.run.localeCompare(b.run))

console.log('run'.padEnd(26), 'n'.padStart(7), 'p95'.padStart(9), 'p99'.padStart(9), '  trend')
const baseline = series[0]?.p95 ?? 0
for (const s of series) {
  const delta = baseline ? ((s.p95 - baseline) / baseline) * 100 : 0
  const trend = delta > 15 ? '🔴 +' + delta.toFixed(0) + '%' : delta < -10 ? '🟢 ' + delta.toFixed(0) + '%' : '🟡'
  console.log(s.run.padEnd(26), String(s.n).padStart(7), s.p95.toFixed(1).padStart(9), s.p99.toFixed(1).padStart(9), ' ', trend)
}
// A drift ratchet: fail if the median p95 of the last 5 runs is more than 20% worse than the first 5.
const recent = series.slice(-5).map((s) => s.p95)
const early = series.slice(0, 5).map((s) => s.p95)
const med = (a) => [...a].sort((x, y) => x - y)[Math.floor(a.length / 2)]
if (early.length && recent.length && med(recent) > med(early) * 1.2) {
  console.error('::error::p95 has regressed more than 20% versus the start of the baseline window')
  process.exit(1)
}
```

**What this script demonstrates.** A staged arrival model where only the hold phase's metrics are treated as steady-state; SLO thresholds as hard gates so the run is a test; business-correctness checks with `correctness_violation` and `amount_mismatch` counters that must be zero, so a fast-but-wrong system fails; generator-health thresholds (`vus`, `http_req_blocked`, `dropped_iterations`) so the numbers are trustworthy; a decline path exercised under load; an early abort on a 10% error rate; environment-driven configuration; a short CI profile; and a drift ratchet instead of a single absolute number.

---

## PROBLEM 8: A Pact contract

**What you must build.** A Pact contract for a `checkout-service` consuming `order-service`, with the consumer test running against a Pact-generated mock, the provider verification job, the provider-state setup endpoint, and the broker configuration with canary deployment tags.

```bash
npm i -D @pact-foundation/pact @pact-foundation/pact-node
```

```ts
// consumer: services/checkout-service/src/infrastructure/order-client.spec.ts
import { PactV3 } from '@pact-foundation/pact'
import { OrderApiClient, NotFoundError } from './order-client'
import { orderFactory } from '@test/factories'

/**
 * The consumer's contract with order-service. Two interactions: the happy path and the
 * not-found path. Matchers are chosen by what THIS consumer's code does with each field.
 */
const pact = new PactV3({
  consumer: 'checkout-service',
  provider: 'order-service',
  version: process.env.GIT_COMMIT_SHA ?? '0.0.1-dev',
  logLevel: process.env.PACT_DEBUG ? 'debug' : 'error',
  ...(process.env.PACT_BROKER_BASE_URL
    ? { pactBrokerUrl: process.env.PACT_BROKER_BASE_URL, pactBrokerToken: process.env.PACT_BROKER_TOKEN }
    : {}),
})

pact
  .given('an order ord_1 exists in status draft with one line item')
  .uponReceiving('a request for order ord_1')
  .withRequest({
    method: 'GET',
    path: '/v1/orders/ord_1',
    headers: { Accept: 'application/vnd.acme.order+json; version=1', Authorization: 'Bearer test-token' },
  })
  .willRespondWith({
    status: 200,
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/vnd.acme.order+json; version=1' },
    body: {
      id: 'ord_1',                                       // equality: the consumer matches on it
      status: 'draft',                                   // equality: the consumer branches on it
      currency: 'GBP',                                   // equality: used in formatting
      subtotalCents: 2500,                               // equality: displayed to the user
      taxCents: 500,
      totalCents: 3000,                                  // equality: the checkout button label
      placedAt: expect.stringMatching(/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T/),   // type only: the consumer never reads the value
      lines: expect.arrayContaining([
        {
          sku: expect.stringMatching(/^[A-Z]{2,10}-\d+$/),      // regex: a real SKU shape, any value
          qty: expect.any(Number),
          unitPriceCents: expect.any(Number),
          taxRateBps: expect.any(Number),
        },
      ]),
      metadata: { source: expect.any(String) },          // an unlisted field: ignored by design
    },
  })

  .given('no order with id ord_missing exists')
  .uponReceiving('a request for order ord_missing')
  .withRequest({ method: 'GET', path: '/v1/orders/ord_missing' })
  .willRespondWith({
    status: 404,
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/problem+json' },
    body: { type: 'https://acme.test/problems/order-not-found', title: 'Order not found', status: 404 },
  })

  .given('order ord_placed exists in status placed')
  .uponReceiving('a request to place order ord_placed')
  .withRequest({ method: 'POST', path: '/v1/orders/ord_placed/actions/place', body: { idempotencyKey: 'idem-1' } })
  .willRespondWith({
    status: 200,
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/vnd.acme.order+json; version=1' },
    body: {
      id: 'ord_placed', status: 'placed', currency: 'GBP',
      subtotalCents: expect.any(Number), taxCents: expect.any(Number), totalCents: expect.any(Number),
      lines: expect.any(Array), placedAt: expect.stringMatching(/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T/),
    },
  })

it('computes the payable total and displays it in the order currency', async () => {
  const handler = pact.handler()
  await handler.start()                       // starts the MOCK provider on a random port

  const client = new OrderApiClient({ baseUrl: handler.url, token: 'test-token' })
  const order = await client.getOrder('ord_1')

  expect(order.totalCents).toBe(3000)
  expect(formatMoney(order.totalCents, order.currency)).toBe('£30.00')
  await handler.stop()
})

it('raises a typed NotFoundError, so the caller can render a 404 page', async () => {
  const handler = pact.handler()
  await handler.start()
  const client = new OrderApiClient({ baseUrl: handler.url, token: 'test-token' })

  await expect(client.getOrder('ord_missing')).rejects.toThrow(NotFoundError)
  await expect(client.getOrder('ord_missing')).rejects.toMatchObject({ status: 404, problemType: expect.stringContaining('order-not-found') })
  await handler.stop()
})

it('places an order and reads the new status', async () => {
  const handler = pact.handler()
  await handler.start()
  const client = new OrderApiClient({ baseUrl: handler.url, token: 'test-token' })

  const placed = await client.place('ord_placed', { idempotencyKey: 'idem-1' })
  expect(placed.status).toBe('placed')
  await handler.stop()
})

it('writes the pact to disk so the provider can verify it', async () => {
  await pact.writePact()
})
```

```ts
// provider: services/order-service/test/pact/verification.spec.ts
import { verifier } from '@pact-foundation/pact'
import { beforeAll, afterAll } from 'vitest'

beforeAll(() => {
  verifier.addSetup({
    providerBaseUrl: process.env.PROVIDER_BASE_URL!,
    // Filter which interactions run: skipping the state-setup POST keeps the trace readable.
    requestFilter: (req, res, next) => {
      if (req.path.startsWith('/__test/state')) return next()
      next()
    },
    stateHandlers: {
      // Optional: handle states in-process instead of over HTTP. Fewer moving parts.
      'an order ord_1 exists in status draft with one line item': async ({ parameters }) => {
        await db.order.upsert({
          where: { id: 'ord_1' },
          create: {
            id: 'ord_1', userId: 'usr_1', status: 'draft', currency: 'GBP',
            subtotalCents: 2500, taxCents: 500, totalCents: 3000,
            lines: { create: [{ sku: 'WIDGET-1', qty: 1, unitPriceCents: 2500, taxRateBps: 2000 }] },
          },
          update: {},
        })
        void parameters
      },
      'no order with id ord_missing exists': async () => {
        await db.order.deleteMany({ where: { id: 'ord_missing' } })
      },
      'order ord_placed exists in status placed': async () => {
        await db.order.upsert({
          where: { id: 'ord_placed' },
          create: { id: 'ord_placed', userId: 'usr_1', status: 'placed', currency: 'GBP', subtotalCents: 1000, taxCents: 200, totalCents: 1200, lines: { create: [{ sku: 'GIZMO-2', qty: 1, unitPriceCents: 1000, taxRateBeps: 2000 }] } },
          update: { status: 'placed' },
        })
      },
    },
  })
})

afterAll(async () => {
  // Non-zero exit fails the provider's build. This is the gate.
  const ok = await verifier.execute({
    provider: 'order-service',
    providerVersion: process.env.GIT_COMMIT_SHA!,
    // Verify against what is actually deployed, plus anything pending release. This is
    // what makes the "what am I about to break?" question answerable.
    consumerVersionSelectors: [
      { tag: 'prod', latest: true },
      { tag: 'main', latest: true },
      { branch: 'main', consumerVersionMatchers: [{ match: '((.*)(?i)-(?:PR-\d+|feature|hotfix|release)-.*)', captureGroups: ['2', '3'] }] },
      { deployedOrRelease: true },
    ],
    pactBrokerUrl: process.env.PACT_BROKER_BASE_URL!,
    pactBrokerToken: process.env.PACT_BROKER_TOKEN,
    providerStatesSetupUrl: process.env.PROVIDER_BASE_URL!,
    logLevel: 'error',
    failIfNoPactsFound: true,      // 🔴 never silently pass because the broker had no pacts
    providerAppVersion: process.env.GIT_COMMIT_SHA,
  })
  if (!ok) process.exit(1)
}, 300_000)
```

```ts
// provider: the HTTP state setup endpoint, for provider versions that do not use in-process handlers.
import { timingSafeEqual } from 'node:crypto'

const EXPECTED = Buffer.from(process.env.PACT_PROVIDER_STATE_KEY ?? '')

export function pactStateGuard(req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) {
  // Mounted only in a verification environment. In production this route does not exist.
  if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production' && process.env.ALLOW_PACT_STATES !== '1') {
    return res.status(404).json({ error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND' } })
  }
  const provided = Buffer.from(String(req.header('x-pact-state-key') ?? ''))
  if (provided.length !== EXPECTED.length || !timingSafeEqual(provided, EXPECTED)) {
    return res.status(403).json({ error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN' } })
  }
  next()
}

export const pactStateRouter = Router()
pactStateRouter.use(pactStateGuard)
pactStateRouter.post('/', async (req, res) => {
  const { state, params } = req.body as { state: string; params?: Record<string, string> }

  // Reset between verifications so a state is never accidentally satisfied by leftovers.
  await db.order.deleteMany({ where: { id: { startsWith: 'ord_' } } })

  switch (state) {
    case 'an order ord_1 exists in status draft with one line item':
      await db.order.create({
        data: {
          id: params?.id ?? 'ord_1', userId: 'usr_1', status: 'draft', currency: 'GBP',
          subtotalCents: 2500, taxCents: 500, totalCents: 3000,
          lines: { create: [{ sku: 'WIDGET-1', qty: 1, unitPriceCents: 2500, taxRateBps: 2000 }] },
        },
      })
      break
    case 'order ord_placed exists in status placed':
      await db.order.create({
        data: {
          id: params?.id ?? 'ord_placed', userId: 'usr_1', status: 'placed', currency: 'GBP',
          subtotalCents: 1000, taxCents: 200, totalCents: 1200,
          lines: { create: [{ sku: 'GIZMO-2', qty: 1, unitPriceCents: 1000, taxRateBps: 2000 }] },
        },
      })
      break
    case 'no order with id ord_missing exists':
      break                                   // the absence IS the state
    default:
      return res.status(500).json({ error: { code: 'UNKNOWN_STATE', state } })
  }

  res.json({ ok: true })
})
```

```ts
// provider: the API must emit the exact media types the consumer's pact declares.
const ORDER_MEDIA_TYPE = 'application/vnd.acme.order+json; version=1'

export const orderRouter = Router()

orderRouter.get('/v1/orders/:id', requireAuth, async (req, res) => {
  const order = await repo.findByIdForUser(req.params.id, req.user.id)   // object-level authz
  if (!order) {
    return res.status(404).type('application/problem+json').json({
      type: 'https://acme.test/problems/order-not-found', title: 'Order not found', status: 404,
    })
  }
  res.type(ORDER_MEDIA_TYPE).json({
    id: order.id, status: order.status, currency: order.currency,
    subtotalCents: order.subtotalCents, taxCents: order.taxCents, totalCents: order.totalCents,
    placedAt: order.placedAt?.toISOString() ?? null,
    lines: order.lines.map((l) => ({ sku: l.sku, qty: l.qty, unitPriceCents: l.unitPriceCents, taxRateBps: l.taxRateBps })),
  })
})
```

```yaml
# .github/workflows/order-service-contract.yml
name: order-service provider verification
on:
  pull_request: { branches: [main] }
  push: { branches: [main] }

jobs:
  verify:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    services:
      postgres:
        image: postgres:16.4-alpine
        env: { POSTGRES_USER: test, POSTGRES_PASSWORD: test, POSTGRES_DB: pact_test }
        ports: ['5432:5432']
        options: --health-cmd "pg_isready -U test" --health-interval 5s --health-timeout 5s --health-retries 12
    env:
      PROVIDER_BASE_URL: http://127.0.0.1:3100
      PACT_BROKER_BASE_URL: ${{ secrets.PACT_BROKER_BASE_URL }}
      PACT_BROKER_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.PACT_BROKER_TOKEN }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm db:migrate
        env: { DATABASE_URL: postgres://test:test@localhost:5432/pact_test }
      - run: pnpm build
      - name: Start the provider
        run: pnpm start &
        env:
          DATABASE_URL: postgres://test:test@localhost:5432/pact_test
          NODE_ENV: verification
          ALLOW_PACT_STATES: '1'
          PACT_PROVIDER_STATE_KEY: ${{ secrets.PACT_PROVIDER_STATE_KEY }}
      - name: Wait for the provider
        run: |
          for i in $(seq 1 60); do
            curl -sf http://127.0.0.1:3100/healthz && break || sleep 1
          done
      - name: Verify every consumer pact
        run: pnpm test:contract
        env: { GIT_COMMIT_SHA: ${{ github.sha }} }
      - name: Publish the Pact matrix as a PR comment
        if: always()
        run: pnpm pact matrix | tee -a "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: failure()
        with: { name: pact-verification, path: services/order-service/pact-logs/ }
```

```ts
// consumer: publishing the pact, tagged so the provider knows which version is deployed
// services/checkout-service/src/infrastructure/publish-pact.ts
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'

if (process.env.CI) {
  const tags = ['main']
  // Only tag a release branch once it is actually deployed.
  if (process.env.DEPLOYED_SHA === process.env.GIT_COMMIT_SHA) tags.push('prod')
  if (process.env.BRANCH?.startsWith('feature/')) tags.push(process.env.BRANCH)

  execFileSync('npx', ['pact-broker', 'publish-pact', '--broker-base-url', process.env.PACT_BROKER_BASE_URL!,
    '--broker-token', process.env.PACT_BROKER_TOKEN!, ...tags.flatMap((t) => ['--tag', t])],
    { stdio: 'inherit' })

  // A build fails if any provider this consumer depends on has not yet verified it.
  execFileSync('npx', ['pact-broker', 'can-i-deploy',
    '--pacticipant', 'checkout-service', '--version', process.env.GIT_COMMIT_SHA!,
    '--to', 'main',
    '--broker-base-url', process.env.PACT_BROKER_BASE_URL!,
    '--broker-token', process.env.PACT_BROKER_TOKEN!,
    '--latest'],
    { stdio: 'inherit' })
}
```

**What this contract demonstrates.** The consumer test running with **no provider deployed**, which is the entire point of consumer-driven contracts; three interactions including the not-found path, which hand-written mocks almost always omit; matcher strictness chosen by what the consumer's code actually does with each field (`equality` for branches, `regex` for shapes, `type` for ignored values); provider verification as a blocking CI gate with `failIfNoPactsFound: true` so an empty broker cannot silently pass; gated provider states that do not exist in production; canary-aware `consumerVersionSelectors` so the provider is verified against what is actually deployed; and `can-i-deploy` as a release gate.

---

## PROBLEM 9: A mutation-testing configuration

**What you must build.** A Stryker configuration that measures whether your tests actually detect bugs, scoped so it is fast enough to run on every PR (changed files only) and exhaustively nightly, with a score threshold, an HTML report, and a baseline for existing debt.

```bash
npm i -D @stryker-mutator/core @stryker-mutator/vitest-runner
```

```jsonc
// stryker.conf.json
{
  "$schema": "./node_modules/@stryker-mutator/core/schema/stryker-schema.json",
  "packageManager": "npm",

  "testRunner": "vitest",
  "reporters": ["html", "clear-text", "progress", "json-summary", "dashboard"],
  "coverageAnalysis": "off",        // vitest already gives us per-file coverage for free
  "incremental": true,              // only mutate files touched since the last run
  "incrementalFile": ".stryker-tmp/incremental.json",
  "cleanTempDir": true,

  // ── What to mutate. The unit and component tiers only: mutation testing a file with
  //    no fast tests produces "escaped mutants" for the whole file, which is noise.
  "mutate": [
    "src/domain/**/*.ts",
    "src/application/**/*.ts",
    "src/lib/**/*.ts",
    "!src/**/*.d.ts",
    "!src/**/index.ts",
    "!src/**/*.{test,spec}.ts",
    "!src/**/types.ts",
    "!src/**/schemas.ts",            // declarative: no logic to mutate
    "!src/**/generated/**",
    "src/ui/**/*.tsx",
    "!src/ui/**/*.stories.tsx",
  ],

  // ── Mutators, in order of preference. The first that can apply wins.
  "mutator": {
    "plugins": ["@stryker-mutator/core"],
    "excludedMutations": [
      "stringLiteral",         // "draft" -> "" is rarely a meaningful bug
      "booleanLiteral",        // covered well by behaviour tests
      "assignmentOperator",
      "propertyRead",          // enormous volume, low signal
      "objectLiteral",
      "arrayDeclaration",
    ],
  },

  // ── The thresholds that matter. Per-directory, so a critical domain file cannot be
  //    rescued by an easy utility.
  "thresholds": {
    "high": 85,
    "low": 70,
    "break": 65,
    "perFile": false
  },
  "thresholds.perMutator": {
    "conditionalExpression": 80,      // boundary and negation mutants: the ones that find real bugs
    "logicalExpression": 80,
    "arithmeticOperator": 80,
    "unaryOperator": 75,
    "optionalChaining": 80,
    "nullishCoalescing": 85,
    "returnValue": 85,
    "blockStatement": 90,
    "equalityOperator": 90,
    "stringLiteralThreshold": 0
  },

  // ── Timeout discipline: a mutant that hangs is a timeout, not a crash.
  "timeoutMS": 5_000,
  "timeoutFactor": 2,
  "testTimeout": 10_000,
  "concurrency": 4,
  "maxConcurrentTestRunners": 4,

  // ── Only the tests that cover the mutated file are run. This is the single biggest
  //    performance lever in Stryker.
  "disableTypeCheck": "src/**/*.ts",
  "disableBail": true,                 // run every mutant even after one fails: complete data
  "forceCoverage": false,

  // ── Ignore a specific mutant without suppressing the whole file.
  "ignoreStatic": true,

  // ── Ignore patterns, each with a reason. An unexplained suppression is hidden test debt.
  "ignorePatterns": [
    { "name": "logger", "source": "app\\.log\\.(debug|info|warn|error)\\(" },
    { "name": "react-render", "source": "return\\s*<[A-Z]" },
    { "name": "exhaustive-switch-default", "source": "default:\\s*\\{?\\s*\\}" },
    { "name": "error-construction", "source": "new\\s+[A-Z]\\w*Error\\(" }
  ],

  // ── Baselines: accept the existing score, then ratchet upward. This is the only way to
  //    adopt mutation testing on a codebase with existing debt.
  "baseline": ".stryker-tmp/mutation-baseline.json",
  "allowNoTests": false,
  "checkRequired": false
}
```

```jsonc
// stryker.diff.json -- the every-PR profile: only what the PR touched, fail only on NEW survivors.
{
  "extends": "./stryker.conf.json",
  "mutate": ["src/domain/**/*.ts", "src/application/**/*.ts"],
  "thresholds": { "high": 80, "low": 70, "break": 70 },
  "reporters": ["clear-text", "html", "json-summary"],
  "ignorePatterns": [
    { "name": "logger", "source": "app\\.log\\." },
    { "name": "error-construction", "source": "new\\s+[A-Z]\\w*Error\\(" }
  ]
}
```

```json
// package.json
{
  "scripts": {
    "test:mutation": "stryker run",
    "test:mutation:diff": "git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD -- 'src/**/*.ts' | sed 's|^|--mutate |' > /tmp/mut.txt; stryker run stryker.diff.json $(cat /tmp/mut.txt) 2>/dev/null || stryker run stryker.diff.json",
    "test:mutation:baseline": "stryker run --baseline",
    "test:mutation:report": "stryker run && npx http-server reports/mutation/html -p 4321"
  }
}
```

```ts
// A module whose mutation score is 100% -- the reference for what "well tested" means.
// Stryker mutates every token; if a mutant survives, there is a missing test.
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import fc from 'fast-check'
import { canTransition, nextStatuses, isTerminal, OrderStatus } from './state-machine'

describe('order state machine', () => {
  const ALL: OrderStatus[] = ['draft', 'placed', 'shipped', 'cancelled', 'refunded']

  // ── Exhaustive truth table. A mutant that flips one entry in this table is caught.
  it('defines the complete transition table', () => {
    expect(
      Object.fromEntries(ALL.flatMap((f) => ALL.map((t) => [`${f}->${t}`, canTransition(f, t)]))),
    ).toMatchInlineSnapshot(`
      {
        "cancelled->cancelled": false,
        "cancelled->draft": false,
        "cancelled->placed": false,
        "cancelled->refunded": false,
        "cancelled->shipped": false,
        "draft->cancelled": true,
        "draft->placed": true,
        "draft->refunded": false,
        "draft->shipped": false,
        "placed->cancelled": true,
        "placed->draft": false,
        "placed->placed": false,
        "placed->refunded": false,
        "placed->shipped": true,
        "refunded->cancelled": false,
        "refunded->draft": false,
        "refunded->placed": false,
        "refunded->refunded": false,
        "refunded->shipped": false,
        "shipped->cancelled": false,
        "shipped->draft": false,
        "shipped->placed": false,
        "shipped->refunded": true,
        "shipped->shipped": false,
      }
    `)
  })

  // ── nextStatuses: catches mutants that change the ORDER or the CONTENT of the result.
  it('returns the reachable statuses in a stable, deterministic order', () => {
    expect(nextStatuses('draft')).toEqual(['placed', 'cancelled'])
    expect(nextStatuses('shipped')).toEqual(['refunded'])
    expect(nextStatuses('refunded')).toEqual([])
    // A property, so adding a status is caught without rewriting the tests.
    fc.assert(
      fc.property(fc.constantFrom(...ALL), (s) => {
        const out = nextStatuses(s)
        expect(new Set(out).size).toBe(out.length)             // no duplicates
        expect(out.every((t) => canTransition(s, t))).toBe(true) // only reachable statuses
        return true
      }),
      { numRuns: 100 },
    )
  })

  // ── isTerminal: a single negation mutant lives or dies here.
  it.each([
    ['draft', false], ['placed', false], ['shipped', false],
    ['cancelled', true], ['refunded', true],
  ])('isTerminal(%s) === %s', (status, expected) => {
    expect(isTerminal(status as OrderStatus)).toBe(expected)
  })
})
```

```ts
// src/domain/state-machine.ts
export type OrderStatus = 'draft' | 'placed' | 'shipped' | 'cancelled' | 'refunded'

const TRANSITIONS: Record<OrderStatus, OrderStatus[]> = {
  draft: ['placed', 'cancelled'],
  placed: ['shipped', 'cancelled'],
  shipped: ['refunded'],
  cancelled: [],
  refunded: [],
}

export function canTransition(from: OrderStatus, to: OrderStatus): boolean {
  return TRANSITIONS[from].includes(to)
}

export function nextStatuses(from: OrderStatus): OrderStatus[] {
  return [...TRANSITIONS[from]]
}

export function isTerminal(status: OrderStatus): boolean {
  return TRANSITIONS[status].length === 0
}
```

```yaml
# .github/workflows/mutation.yml
name: Mutation testing
on:
  pull_request:
  schedule: [{ cron: '41 2 * * *' }]

jobs:
  mutation-diff:
    if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with: { fetch-depth: 0 }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm test:mutation:diff
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: always()
        with: { name: mutation-report, path: reports/mutation/html, retention-days: 14 }
      - name: Post the score to the PR
        if: always()
        run: cat reports/mutation/mutation-summary.json | node scripts/pr-comment.mjs

  mutation-full:
    if: github.event_name == 'schedule'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 90
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm test:mutation
      - run: pnpm test:mutation:baseline      # writes the new baseline, failing if the score did not rise
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with: { name: mutation-full-report, path: reports/mutation/html, retention-days: 30 }
```

```js
// scripts/pr-comment.mjs -- a surviving-mutant summary in the PR, which is what makes
// mutation testing actionable rather than just a number.
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'

const s = JSON.parse(readFileSync('reports/mutation/mutation-summary.json', 'utf8'))
const lines = [
  '### Mutation testing',
  '',
  `| Metric | Score |`,
  `| --- | --- |`,
  `| Mutation score | ${(s.mutationScore * 100).toFixed(1)}% |`,
  `| Covered code | ${(s.coveredCode * 100).toFixed(1)}% |`,
  `| Mutants generated | ${s.mutationScoreBasedOnCoveredCode === undefined ? '—' : ''} |`,
  `| Killed | ${s.killed} |`,
  `| Survived | ${s.survived} |`,
  `| Timeout | ${s.timeout} |`,
  `| No coverage | ${s.noCoverage} |`,
]
if (s.survived > 0) {
  lines.push('', '<details><summary>Surviving mutants (these are missing tests)</details>', '')
  for (const m of (s.survivedMutations ?? []).slice(0, 20)) {
    lines.push(`- \`${m.location.file}:${m.location.start.line}\` — ${m.mutatorName} — \`${m.replacement}\``)
  }
  lines.push('', '</details>')
}
console.log(lines.join('\n'))
```

**What this configuration demonstrates.** Mutation testing scoped to files with fast tests, because mutating a file whose only tests are integration ones produces a wall of meaningless escaped mutants; per-mutator thresholds that weight the *informative* mutants (conditionals, boundaries, nullish coalescing) above the noisy ones (property reads, object literals); `ignorePatterns` each with a reason; a baseline so the existing score is accepted and then ratcheted; two profiles -- changed-files-only on every PR and full nightly; and a PR comment listing the surviving mutants, which is what turns the number into a work list.

---

## PROBLEM 10: A complete CI testing pipeline

**What you must build.** A full pipeline: fast gate, integration with real services, build-once artifact reuse, sharded E2E against a preview environment, accessibility, performance, mutation, security scanning, a merge-gate aggregator, and a nightly matrix.

```yaml
# .github/workflows/ci.yml -- the whole testing stack in one file.
name: CI

on:
  pull_request:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  schedule:
    - { cron: '17 3 * * 1-5' }          # weekdays at 03:17: the nightly matrix

concurrency:
  group: ci-${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}
  cancel-in-progress: true

permissions:
  contents: read
  security-events: write
  checks: write
  pull-requests: write

env:
  NODE_VERSION: '22.12.0'
  PNPM_VERSION: '9.15.0'

jobs:
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # STAGE 1: the fast gate. Target < 3 minutes. Hard required.
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  verify:
    name: Lint · Types · Unit · Property · Mutation(diff)
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 12
    outputs:
      coverage-summary: ${{ steps.cov.outputs.summary }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with: { fetch-depth: 0, persist-credentials: false }

      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
        with: { version: ${{ env.PNPM_VERSION }} }
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: ${{ env.NODE_VERSION }}
          cache: pnpm

      - name: Install
        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile

      - name: Lint (ESLint + dependency boundaries)
        run: |
          pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings 0
          pnpm exec depcruise src --config dependency-cruiser.config.mjs

      - name: Typecheck
        run: pnpm typecheck

      - name: Unit + component tests with coverage
        id: cov
        run: |
          pnpm test -- --coverage --reporter=default --reporter=json-summary --reporter=junit --outputFile.junit=test-results/unit.xml

      - name: Diff coverage gate (90% of changed lines)
        run: |
          pnpm exec diff-cover coverage/lcov.info \
            --compare-branch="origin/${{ github.base_ref || 'main' }}" \
            --fail-under=90 \
            --markdown-report diff-cover.md

      - name: Type-level tests
        run: pnpm exec vitest run --typecheck --typecheck.only

      - name: Property tests
        run: pnpm test:property

      - name: Mutation testing on changed files only
        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'
        run: pnpm test:mutation:diff

      - name: PR description check
        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'
        run: node scripts/pr-description-check.mjs
        env: { PR_BODY: ${{ github.event.pull_request.body }} }

      - name: Post coverage + mutation to the job summary
        if: always()
        run: |
          {
            echo "### Coverage"; cat coverage/coverage-summary.json | jq -r 'to_entries[] | select(.key!="total") | "| \(.key) | \(.value.lines.pct)% | \(.value.branches.pct)% |"'
            echo; echo "### Diff coverage"; cat diff-cover.md 2>/dev/null || echo "_not produced_"
          } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"

      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: always()
        with: { name: coverage, path: '{coverage,diff-cover.md,test-results}', retention-days: 14 }

  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # STAGE 2: integration, with real Postgres, Redis and RabbitMQ. Parallel with the
  # build, because it does not depend on it.
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  integration:
    name: Integration
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 20
    services:
      postgres:
        image: postgres:16.4-alpine
        env: { POSTGRES_USER: test, POSTGRES_PASSWORD: test, POSTGRES_DB: app_test }
        ports: ['5432:5432']
        options: >-
          --health-cmd "pg_isready -U test -d app_test"
          --health-interval 5s --health-timeout 5s --health-retries 12
      redis:
        image: redis:7.4-alpine
        ports: ['6379:6379']
        options: >-
          --health-cmd "redis-cli ping" --health-interval 5s --health-timeout 5s --health-retries 12
      rabbitmq:
        image: rabbitmq:3.13-alpine
        ports: ['5672:5672']
        options: >-
          --health-cmd "rabbitmq-diagnostics -q ping"
          --health-interval 10s --health-timeout 10s --health-retries 12
    env:
      TEST_DATABASE_URL: postgres://test:test@localhost:5432/app_test
      TEST_REDIS_URL: redis://localhost:6379
      TEST_RABBIT_URL: amqp://guest:guest@localhost:5672
      CI: '1'
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with: { fetch-depth: 0 }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
        with: { version: ${{ env.PNPM_VERSION }} }
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: ${{ env.NODE_VERSION }}, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile

      - name: Migrations (the real path, not db push)
        run: pnpm exec prisma migrate deploy
        env: { DATABASE_URL: ${{ env.TEST_DATABASE_URL }} }

      - name: Integration suite
        run: pnpm test:integration -- --reporter=default --reporter=junit --outputFile.junit=test-results/integration.xml

      - name: Contract verification
        run: pnpm test:contract
        env:
          PROVIDER_BASE_URL: http://127.0.0.1:3100
          PACT_BROKER_BASE_URL: ${{ secrets.PACT_BROKER_BASE_URL }}
          PACT_BROKER_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.PACT_BROKER_TOKEN }}
          GIT_COMMIT_SHA: ${{ github.sha }}

      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: always()
        with: { name: integration-diagnostics, path: test-artifacts, retention-days: 7 }

  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # STAGE 3: build ONCE. Every downstream stage reuses the artefact.
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  build:
    name: Build
    needs: [verify]
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 12
    outputs:
      image: ${{ steps.meta.outputs.image }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
        with: { version: ${{ env.PNPM_VERSION }} }
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: ${{ env.NODE_VERSION }}, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile

      - name: Build
        run: pnpm build
      - name: Type-artefact checks (size budget, no test code in the bundle)
        run: |
          node scripts/check-bundle.mjs --max-gzip 220000
          # A test-only import shipping to production is a security and size bug.
          if grep -rlE "(from ['\"]\.\.?/.*(test-support|\.spec|\.test))" dist/ ; then
            echo "::error::Test-only code found in the production bundle"; exit 1
          fi
      - name: Container image
        run: |
          docker buildx build --platform linux/amd64 \
            -t ${{ steps.meta.outputs.image }} --load .
        id: meta
        env:
          IMAGE: ghcr.io/acme/api
      - name: Write the image ref
        run: echo "image=ghcr.io/acme/api:${{ github.sha }}" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with: { name: dist, path: dist, retention-days: 3 }

  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # STAGE 4: E2E against a preview environment built from the PR artefact.
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  e2e:
    name: E2E (shard ${{ matrix.shard }}/${{ matrix.total }})
    needs: [build, integration]
    if: >-
      github.event_name == 'push' ||
      contains(github.event.pull_request.labels.*.name, 'e2e') ||
      contains(github.event.pull_request.labels.*.name, 'user-facing')
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 30
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false                  # never cancel a sibling: its trace is the evidence
      matrix:
        shard: [1, 2, 3, 4]
        total: [4]
    env:
      RUN_ID: ${{ github.run_id }}-${{ github.run_attempt }}-${{ matrix.shard }}
      E2E_WORKERS: 2
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
        with: { version: ${{ env.PNPM_VERSION }} }
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: ${{ env.NODE_VERSION }}, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile

      - uses: actions/download-artifact@v4
        with: { name: dist, path: dist }

      - name: Cache the Playwright browsers (seconds, not minutes)
        id: playwright-cache
        uses: actions/cache@v4
        with:
          path: ~/.cache/ms-playwright
          key: pw-${{ runner.os }}-${{ hashFiles('pnpm-lock.yaml') }}

      - name: Install browsers
        if: steps.playwright-cache.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'
        run: pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps chromium webkit

      - name: Deploy the preview environment from this PR's image
        id: preview
        uses: acme/deploy-preview@v3
        with:
          image: ${{ needs.build.outputs.image }}
          migrate: true
          waitFor: /healthz

      - name: E2E setup (migrations, reference data, admin storage state)
        run: pnpm e2e:global-setup
        env:
          E2E_BASE_URL: ${{ steps.preview.outputs.url }}
          E2E_KEY: ${{ secrets.E2E_KEY }}
          E2E_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.E2E_TOKEN }}
          EXPECTED_API_VERSION: v1

      - name: Run the shard
        run: |
          pnpm exec playwright test \
            --project=chromium --project=webkit \
            --shard=${{ matrix.shard }}/${{ matrix.total }}
        env:
          E2E_BASE_URL: ${{ steps.preview.outputs.url }}
          E2E_KEY: ${{ secrets.E2E_KEY }}
          E2E_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.E2E_TOKEN }}

      - name: Publish the HTML report (always, so a green shard is still inspectable)
        if: always()
        run: pnpm exec playwright merge-reports ./playwright-report || true

      - name: Flake budget check
        if: always()
        run: node scripts/flake-budget.mjs test-results/e2e-results.json

      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: always()
        with:
          name: e2e-report-${{ matrix.shard }}
          path: '{playwright-report,e2e/.artifacts,test-results}'
          retention-days: 7

      - name: Destroy the preview environment
        if: always()
        uses: acme/deploy-preview-teardown@v3

  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # STAGE 5: accessibility, as a first-class gate.
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  a11y:
    name: Accessibility
    needs: [build]
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 15
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
        with: { version: ${{ env.PNPM_VERSION }} }
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: ${{ env.NODE_VERSION }}, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - name: axe over the critical pages, on the assembled application
        run: pnpm e2e:a11y
        env:
          E2E_BASE_URL: ${{ needs.preview-url.outputs.url }}
          E2E_KEY: ${{ secrets.E2E_KEY }}
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: always()
        with: { name: a11y-report, path: reports/a11y, retention-days: 14 }

  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # STAGE 6: performance gate against the preview environment.
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  performance:
    name: Performance
    needs: [build]
    if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 15
    container: grafana/k6:latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: k6 smoke profile
        run: |
          k6 run --quiet \
            -e BASE_URL="${{ env.PREVIEW_URL }}" \
            -e TOKEN="${{ secrets.E2E_TOKEN }}" \
            -e E2E_KEY="${{ secrets.E2E_KEY }}" \
            --out json=perf/results/${{ github.run_id }}.json \
            perf/smoke.js
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with: { name: perf-results, path: perf/results, retention-days: 30 }

  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # STAGE 7: security scanning. The ratchet policy, not a raw report.
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  security:
    name: Security
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 20
    permissions:
      contents: read
      security-events: write
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with: { fetch-depth: 0 }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
        with: { version: ${{ env.PNPM_VERSION }} }
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: ${{ env.NODE_VERSION }}, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile

      - name: Semgrep (tuned ruleset + project rules)
        uses: returntocorp/semgrep-action@v1
        with: { config: p/semgrep-rule-misconfiguration, generateSarif: true }
        env: { SEMGREP_APP_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SEMGREP_APP_TOKEN }} }

      - name: CodeQL
        uses: github/codeql-action/init@v3
        with: { languages: typescript,javascript, queries: security-extended }
      - uses: github/codeql-action/analyze@v3

      - name: Dependency vulnerabilities
        run: |
          pnpm audit --audit-level=critical --json > audit.json || true
          node scripts/osv-policy.mjs audit.json
          node scripts/dependency-licence-check.mjs

      - name: Secret scanning (tree + full history)
        run: |
          docker run --rm -v "$PWD:/repo" zricethezav/gitleaks:latest detect \
            --source=/repo --redact --exit-code 1

      - name: Test data must be obviously synthetic
        run: node scripts/check-test-data.mjs

      - name: Container scan
        run: trivy image --severity HIGH,CRITICAL --exit-code 1 --ignore-unfixed ghcr.io/acme/api:${{ github.sha }}

      - name: IaC scan
        run: trivy fs --scanners misconfig --severity HIGH,CRITICAL --exit-code 1 .

      - name: Enforce the security ratchet
        run: node scripts/security-policy.mjs --ratchet --max-new 0

      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: always()
        with: { name: security-reports, path: '{audit.json,reports}', retention-days: 30 }

  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # STAGE 8: the merge gate. One job whose result expresses the actual policy.
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  required:
    name: Required checks
    needs: [verify, integration, build, security]
    if: always()
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Evaluate
        run: |
          cat > "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY" <<'EOF'
          | Stage | Result |
          | --- | --- |
          | verify | ${{ needs.verify.result }} |
          | integration | ${{ needs.integration.result }} |
          | build | ${{ needs.build.result }} |
          | e2e | ${{ needs.e2e.result }} |
          | a11y | ${{ needs.a11y.result }} |
          | security | ${{ needs.security.result }} |
          EOF
          fail=0
          for stage in verify integration build security; do
            result="${{ needs.$stage.result }}"
            if [ "$result" != "success" ]; then
              echo "::error::Stage $stage did not succeed: $result"
              fail=1
            fi
          done

          # E2E and a11y are advisory on PRs unless the change is user-facing, and always
          # required on main. The label and the event type decide.
          if [ "${{ github.event_name }}" = "push" ]; then
            for stage in e2e a11y; do
              [ "${{ needs.$stage.result }}" = "success" ] || { echo "::error::Stage $stage: ${{ needs.$stage.result }}"; fail=1; }
            done
          else
            labels='${{ join(github.event.pull_request.labels.*.name, ',') }}'
            if [[ "$labels" == *"e2e"* || "$labels" == *"user-facing"* ]]; then
              for stage in e2e a11y; do
                [ "${{ needs.$stage.result }}" = "success" ] || { echo "::error::Stage $stage: ${{ needs.$stage.result }}"; fail=1; }
              done
            else
              echo "::notice::E2E and a11y were advisory for this change (no user-facing label)."
            fi
          fi
          exit $fail

  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # STAGE 9: the nightly matrix. Everything expensive, on a schedule.
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  nightly:
    name: Nightly matrix
    if: github.event_name == 'schedule'
    needs: [verify]
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 90
    env:
      RUN_ID: nightly-${{ github.run_id }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with: { fetch-depth: 0 }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
        with: { version: ${{ env.PNPM_VERSION }} }
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: ${{ env.NODE_VERSION }}, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps chromium firefox webkit

      - name: Full E2E across all browsers and devices
        run: pnpm exec playwright test

      - name: Full visual regression, baselines regenerated and diffed
        run: |
          pnpm exec playwright test --grep @visual || true
          node scripts/visual-report.mjs

      - name: Full mutation testing with a rising baseline
        run: pnpm test:mutation && pnpm test:mutation:baseline

      - name: Full soak test (4 hours at 70% of peak)
        run: k6 run -e BASE_URL=$STAGING_URL -e THINK_TIME=1 perf/checkout.js
        continue-on-error: true

      - name: DAST against an isolated environment
        run: |
          docker run --rm -v "$PWD:/zap/wrk" -t ghcr.io/zaproxy/zaproxy:stable \
            zap-baseline.py -t $STAGING_URL -r zap-report.html -J zap.json || true
          node scripts/dast-policy.mjs zap.json

      - name: Dependency freshness and provider smoke tests
        run: |
          pnpm outdated --long || true
          pnpm test:provider-smoke            # real sandbox calls; shape drift fails here

      - name: Test data janitor
        run: node scripts/janitor.ts --env staging --max-age-hours 24

      - name: Publish the nightly summary
        if: always()
        run: node scripts/nightly-summary.mjs >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"

      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: always()
        with: { name: nightly-reports, path: reports, retention-days: 30 }
```

```js
// scripts/flake-budget.mjs -- the metric that keeps the suite honest.
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'

const path = process.argv[2]
const report = JSON.parse(readFileSync(path, 'utf8'))
const specs = report.suites.flatMap((s) => s.specs)
const total = specs.length
const retried = specs.filter((s) => s.tests.some((t) => t.results.length > 1))
const rate = total ? (retried.length / total) * 100 : 0
const budget = Number(process.env.FLAKE_BUDGET ?? 0.5)

console.log(`::notice::flake rate ${rate.toFixed(2)}% (${retried.length}/${total}) — budget ${budget}%`)
if (rate > budget) {
  console.error(`::error::Flake budget exceeded. These tests passed only on retry:`)
  for (const s of retried) console.error(`  - ${s.file}:${s.line} ${s.title}`)
  process.exit(1)
}
```

```yaml
# .pre-commit-config.yaml -- the feedback loop, kept under three seconds.
repos:
  - repo: https://github.com/pre-commit/pre-commit-hooks
    rev: v4.6.0
    hooks:
      - id: check-added-large-files
        args: ['--maxkb=1024']
      - id: detect-private-key
      - id: end-of-file-fixer
      - id: trailing-whitespace
      - id: check-merge-conflict
  - repo: https://github.com/gitleaks/gitleaks
    rev: v8.18.4
    hooks:
      - id: gitleaks
  - repo: local
    hooks:
      # Fast only. The full type-aware set belongs in CI.
      - id: eslint-fast
        name: eslint (type-unaware, fast)
        entry: pnpm exec eslint --no-eslintrc-config --rule '{}' .
        language: system
        pass_filenames: true
        types: [ts, tsx]
        args: [--config eslint.fast.config.js]
      - id: tsc-on-staged
        name: tsc --noEmit
        entry: pnpm exec tsc --noEmit
        language: system
        files: ^(src|test)/
        types: [ts, tsx]
        pass_filenames: false
```

**What this pipeline demonstrates.** Stages ordered by value per second; the merge gate expressed as one aggregator job that reads actual job results, with E2E and a11y advisory on PRs unless the change is user-facing and always required on main; a build-once artefact reused by every downstream stage; sharded E2E with `fail-fast: false` so sibling traces survive; native CI service containers with health checks rather than Testcontainers for the standard engines; a flake-budget gate that fails the build when tests pass only on retry; a security ratchet that fails on *new* high/critical findings and on stale baseline entries; a nightly stage that runs only the expensive things (full browsers, full mutation, soak, DAST, provider smoke, janitor); and a pre-commit hook set kept fast enough to actually be used.

---

# PHASE 9 DEBUGGING SCENARIOS (12)

> Twelve realistic failures, each presented as: symptom, how to reproduce, how to diagnose, the root cause, the fix, and the guardrail that stops it recurring. These are the failures that actually happen.

---

## SCENARIO 1: The unit test that passes alone and fails in the suite

**Symptom.** `pricing.spec.ts` passes in isolation (`npx vitest run src/domain/pricing.spec.ts`) and about 1 in 8 times in the full suite, with a failure like `expected 2500 to be 3000` on a discount calculation.

**Reproduction.**
```bash
npx vitest run --repeat.each 200
npx vitest run --sequence.shuffle          # order changes the outcome: a smoking gun
npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism      # passes serially: confirms shared state, not timing
```

**Diagnosis.** Run the file alone with `--sequence.shuffle --repeat.each 200` and log which other file ran immediately before. The failure correlates with a specific predecessor, which is the whole investigation. Concretely, the usual culprits are:
- a module-level mutable object in a *production* file (`let cache = new Map()` at module scope) that another test populated;
- a test that mutates a shared fixture object imported from a factory module;
- `vi.mock` of a module in one file leaking into another because the module registry is shared when `isolate: false`;
- a real `Date` mutation: a test calls `vi.setSystemTime(...)` and a sibling reads `Date.now()`;
- a module-level `process.env` write with no `vi.unstubAllEnvs()`.

**Root cause (in this case).** `src/domain/pricing.ts` had a module-level memo:
```ts
// 🔴 Module-level cache: state that outlives a single test run of this file.
const tierCache = new Map<string, number>()
export function resolveTier(subtotalCents: number) {
  const key = String(subtotalCents)
  const hit = tierCache.get(key)
  if (hit !== undefined) return hit
  const tier = computeTier(subtotalCents)
  tierCache.set(key, tier)
  return tier
}
```
A previous test file called `resolveTier(2500)` before a promotion-expired scenario invalidated the cache, and the stale entry survived.

**Fix.**
```ts
// ✅ No hidden state. The memo, if it is genuinely needed, is a dependency with a lifetime.
export function resolveTier(subtotalCents: number, tiers: Tier[], now: Date): Tier {
  return tiers
    .filter((t) => t.minSubtotalCents <= subtotalCents && t.activeFrom <= now && t.activeTo > now)
    .sort((a, b) => b.minSubtotalCents - a.minSubtotalCents)[0] ?? tiers[0]!
}
```
Making it a pure function removed the state entirely, which is the correct answer: the cache was not buying anything measurable, and it was a correctness hazard in production too (a promotion change would not be picked up for the TTL of the process).

**Guardrail.**
1. `sequence.shuffle` in CI for the unit tier, with a fixed seed, so order dependence surfaces on the pipeline rather than on a colleague's machine.
2. A lint rule banning module-level `let`/`const Map`/`Set` in `src/domain/**`.
3. `test/setup-global.ts` calling `vi.resetModules()`, `vi.restoreAllMocks()`, `vi.unstubAllEnvs()` in `beforeEach` (see Problem 1).
4. A test that asserts purity directly:
```ts
it('resolveTier is pure: the same inputs always give the same output', () => {
  const first = resolveTier(2500, tiers, new Date('2026-03-01'))
  resolveTier(999_999, tiers, new Date('2027-01-01'))    // poison any hypothetical cache
  expect(resolveTier(2500, tiers, new Date('2026-03-01'))).toEqual(first)
})
```

---

## SCENARIO 2: The async test that hangs until the timeout, with no error

**Symptom.** `checkout.test.tsx` fails after 5 seconds with `Test timed out in 5000ms.` and an empty stack. No assertion error, no unhandled rejection.

**Reproduction.**
```bash
npx vitest run src/ui/checkout.test.tsx --testTimeout 3000 --reporter=verbose
```

**Diagnosis.** A timeout with no error is almost always one of three things, in this order of likelihood:

1. **A promise chain that can never advance**, because fake timers faked the microtask pump. Symptom: the test only hangs when `vi.useFakeTimers()` is active.
2. **A `waitFor` that is waiting for something the app never does** (a spinner that never resolves because the query is in `error` state, not `loading`).
3. **An event listener the app registers but the test never triggers**, so a state machine never leaves its first state.

**Proof.** Add instrumentation, do not guess:
```ts
it('shows the confirmation after paying', async ({ page }) => {
  const log: string[] = []
  const origFetch = globalThis.fetch
  globalThis.fetch = async (...a) => { log.push(`fetch ${a[0]}`); const r = await origFetch(...a); log.push(`-> ${r.status}`); return r }
  const t = setTimeout(() => { console.error('STUCK. DOM:', document.body.innerHTML.slice(0, 2000), '\nNET:', log) }, 2_000)
  try {
    await shopper.checkout().payWithCard(CARD).placeOrder()
  } finally { clearTimeout(t) }
})
```
The DOM snapshot and the network log at the moment of the hang tell you immediately which state the UI is in.

**Root cause (in this case).** The test file's `beforeAll` called `vi.useFakeTimers()` globally "because the component has a spinner", and the component's submit path awaited a debounced `setTimeout` inside a `useEffect`. With `setTimeout` faked and never advanced, the promise never resolved. The spinner was also driven by a real timer.

**Fix.**
```ts
// ✅ Fake timers only where they are needed, and drive them with the ASYNC variant
//    so the microtask queue is drained between timer callbacks.
it('pays and shows the confirmation', async () => {
  const user = userEvent.setup({ advanceTimers: vi.advanceTimersByTime })
  render(<Checkout />)
  await user.type(screen.getByRole('textbox', { name: /card/i }), '4242')
  await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: /pay/i }))

  await expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: /thank you/i })).toBeVisible({ timeout: 5_000 })
  // No timer manipulation at all: this test has no reason to fake time.
})

// ✅ Where a timer IS the subject, advance it explicitly and asynchronously.
it('shows "processing" while the payment is in flight', async () => {
  vi.useFakeTimers()
  render(<Checkout />)
  await act(async () => { await user.click(payButton) })
  expect(screen.getByRole('status')).toHaveTextContent(/processing/i)
  await act(async () => { await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(3_000) })
  expect(screen.queryByRole('status')).not.toBeInTheDocument()
  vi.useRealTimers()
})
```

**Guardrail.**
1. Never call `vi.useFakeTimers()` in `beforeAll`. Scope it to the individual tests that need it, and restore in `afterEach`.
2. Narrow the faked API surface in config: `toFake: ['setTimeout', 'clearTimeout', 'setInterval', 'clearInterval', 'Date']`. Do **not** fake `queueMicrotask`, `nextTick`, or `performance`.
3. The timer-leak guard in `test/setup-timers.ts` (Problem 1) turns "hung with no error" into "5 timers still pending", which is actionable.
4. A `testTimeout` of 5s with a comment that it is a bug indicator, so a hang is a red build and not a mystery.

---

## SCENARIO 3: The integration test that fails only on CI, and only sometimes

**Symptom.** `orders.api.test.ts` fails on CI roughly 6% of runs with `P2028: Transaction API error: Transaction already closed` or `P1001: Can't reach database server`. Locally it is 100% green.

**Reproduction.**
```bash
# Reproduce locally by creating the pressure CI creates.
npx vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.ts --maxWorkers=16   # CI uses 4; go higher
DEBUG_SQL=1 npx vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.ts --repeat.each 20
```

**Diagnosis.** `P1001: Can't reach database server` on a containerised Postgres with a healthy health check almost always means **the connection pool**, not the server. The evidence:
- `docker logs` on the Postgres container during the failure: look for `too many clients already`, or nothing at all (meaning the connection never arrived, i.e. the pool was exhausted client-side).
- Prisma logs at `warn`/`info`: the pool's `connection_limit` versus `pool_timeout`.
- The failures correlate with worker count: raising `maxWorkers` raises the failure rate proportionally.
- The failures cluster at the *start* of the run, when every worker is opening its pool at once.

**Root cause.** Two causes stacked:
1. `maxWorkers: 8` in the integration config against a Postgres container with `max_connections = 100`, but Prisma's *default* pool per worker is `num_cpus * 2 + 1`. On a 4-core runner that is 9 connections per worker, so 8 workers x 9 = 72, plus the setup connection, plus health checks, and a single test that leaks a transaction pushes it over. Worse, the `VITEST_WORKER_ID`-based database cloning meant 8 separate databases, each needing its own pool, on a container sized for the default 100.
2. A test that opened a transaction and never released it (an early `return` inside `withRolledBackDb` before the sentinel throw), so a connection stayed checked out for the rest of the worker.

**Fix.**
```ts
// ✅ Size the pool to the worker count, and set it explicitly on the transactional client.
const WORKERS = Number(process.env.CI_WORKERS ?? 4)
const CONNECTIONS_PER_WORKER = 5

export async function inTransaction<T>(fn: (trx: PrismaClient) => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
  const prisma = getPrisma()
  const box: { value?: T } = {}
  await prisma.$transaction(
    async (trx) => {
      box.value = await fn(trx)          // if `fn` throws, the transaction unwinds AND is released
      throw new RollbackSignal()          // only reached on success
    },
    {
      maxWait: 10_000,                    // fail fast rather than queue indefinitely
      timeout: 20_000,
      isolationLevel: 'ReadCommitted',
    },
  ).catch((e) => { if (e instanceof RollbackSignal) return; throw e })
  return box.value as T
}

function getPrisma(): PrismaClient {
  return new PrismaClient({
    datasources: { db: { url: TEST_DATABASE_URL } },
    // Never rely on the cpu-derived default in CI.
    // @ts-expect-error -- pool sizing is not in the generated types
    datasourceUrl: TEST_DATABASE_URL,
  }).$extends({
    query: { $allModels: { async $allOperations({ query, operation, model }) {
      return query(query)
    } } },
  })
}
```
And in the harness, one client per worker, created lazily and reused:
```ts
let workerPrisma: PrismaClient | undefined
export function getWorkerPrisma(): PrismaClient {
  workerPrisma ??= new PrismaClient({
    log: process.env.DEBUG_SQL ? ['warn', 'error', { emit: 'event', level: 'query' }] : ['error'],
  })
  return workerPrisma
}
```
Plus in CI, size the database:
```yaml
services:
  postgres:
    image: postgres:16.4-alpine
    env: { POSTGRES_USER: test, POSTGRES_PASSWORD: test, POSTGRES_DB: app_test }
    ports: ['5432:5432']
    options: >-
      --health-cmd "pg_isready -U test -d app_test"
      --health-interval 5s --health-timeout 5s --health-retries 12
    # Raise max_connections explicitly, and log when we approach the limit.
    #
    # A cleaner alternative: use a *pooler* (pgbouncer in transaction mode) between
    # the tests and Postgres. In transaction mode, transaction-per-test is exactly the
    # right access pattern, so pgbouncer's transaction pooling is compatible and lets
    # you run hundreds of logical workers over ~20 real connections.
```

**Guardrail.**
1. Set the pool size explicitly. Never rely on a cpu-derived default in CI.
2. `maxWorkers` for integration must be a conscious number tied to the pool, not a CPU count.
3. Assert in the harness that no connection is left checked out after each test:
```ts
afterEach(async () => {
  const prisma = getWorkerPrisma()
  const { rows } = await prisma.$queryRawUnsafe<{ n: string }[]>(`
    SELECT count(*)::text AS n FROM pg_stat_activity WHERE datname = current_database() AND state = 'idle in transaction'`)
  if (Number(rows[0]!.n) > 0) throw new Error(`${rows[0]!.n} connection(s) left idle in transaction -- a test leaked a transaction`)
})
```
4. Add a query timeout so a hung query fails rather than blocking the pool: `SET LOCAL statement_timeout = '10s'` inside each test transaction.

## SCENARIO 4: The component test that fails on `getByRole` with "found multiple elements"

**Symptom.** After a redesign that added a mobile layout alongside the desktop one, 22 component tests fail with:
```
TestingLibraryElementError: Found multiple elements with the role "button" and name "Save"
```
Some of them pass and some fail, depending on the viewport the test set.

**Reproduction.**
```bash
npx vitest run --project component src/ui
```

**Diagnosis.** Two distinct bugs are hiding behind one symptom.

1. **Duplicate DOM.** The redesign renders both a desktop and a mobile header, and CSS hides one. In jsdom, `display: none` is not applied by the external stylesheet at all, so **both are "visible" to Testing Library**. This is the key insight people miss: jsdom does not apply your CSS, so a visually-hidden element is queryable.
2. **The tests are asserting on the wrong thing.** A button labelled "Save" appears in the profile dialog, the billing form, and the notification drawer. The test means one specific button.

**Root cause.** The component duplicated markup for responsive layout, and the tests used a broad role+name query that is not unique in the product.

**Fix.** Fix the component, not the tests -- but fix the tests too, because the duplication is a design problem and the broad query is a test problem.

